GOVERNMENT OF INDIA 
MINISTRY OF CONSUMER AFFAIRS, FOOD & PUBLIC DISTRIBUTION 
DEPARTMENT OF FOOD AND PUBLIC DISTRIBUTION 
 
RAJYA SABHA 
UNSTARRED QUESTION NO.1515 
TO BE ANSWERED ON 29TH JULY, 2022 
 
CORRELATION OF NATIONAL FOOD SECURITY ACT AND POVERTY  
 
1515    DR. SASMIT PATRA:  
 
Will the Minister of CONSUMER AFFAIRS, FOOD AND PUBLIC DISTRIBUTION 
be pleased to state: 
 
(a)  whether  food  security  provided  through  National  Food  Security  Act  (NFSA)  is  a  
scheme  which  correlates  to  poverty  in  the  country  or  it  is a programme to provide social  
security to majority of the population of the country;  
 
(b)  whether  NFSA has only poor people under its coverage or it is a programme which also 
includes the Above Poverty Line (APL) people for food security as a national mandate; and  
 
(c)  the  reasons  why  NFSA  is  thought  to  be  a poverty alleviation programme when it is  
more of a social security programme? 
 
 
A N S W E R 
MINISTER OF STATE FOR MINISTRY OF RURAL DEVELOPMENT AND 
CONSUMER AFFAIRS, FOOD & PUBLIC DISTRIBUTION  
(SADHVI NIRANJAN JYOTI) 
 
(a) to (c):  The National Food Security Act, 2013 (NFSA) provides for coverage of upto 75% 
of the rural and upto 50% of the urban population for receiving highly subsidized foodgrains 
under Targeted Public Distribution System (TPDS), which at Census 2011 comes to about 
81.35 crore. The Coverage under the Act has been delinked from the poverty estimates and is 
substantially high to ensure that all the vulnerable and needy sections of the society get its 
benefit.  
 
Identification of beneficiaries under the Act is under two categories- households 
covered under Antyodaya Anna Yojana (AAY) which constitute poorest of the poor, to the 
extent specified by the Central Government and the remaining households as priority 
households (PHH) to be identified by the State Governments/ UT Administrations as per 
criteria evolved by them, within the coverage determined for the State/UT. Thus, the criteria 
for identification of priority households may vary from State to State depending upon their 
local requirement. There is no Above Poverty Line (APL) category under the Act. 
.........2/- 
159

 
-2- 
 
 
Under the Act, while Antyodaya Anna Yojana (AAY) households that constitute the 
poorest of the poor are legally entitled to receive 35 kg of foodgrains per family per month, 
priority households are entitled to 5 kg per person per month. 
 
One of the guiding principles of the Act is its life cycle approach wherein special 
provisions have been made for pregnant women and lactating mothers and children in the age 
group of 6 months to 14 years, by entitling them to receive nutritious meal free of cost 
through a widespread network of Integrated Child Development Services (ICDS) centres, 
called Anganwadi Centres under ICDS scheme and also through schools under Mid-Day 
Meal (MDM) scheme. Higher nutritional norms have been prescribed for malnourished 
children upto 6 years of age. Pregnant women and lactating mothers are further entitled to 
receive cash maternity benefit under Pradhan Mantri Matru Vandana Yojana (PMMVY) to 
partly compensate for the wage loss during the period of pregnancy and also to supplement 
nutrition as per approved norms.

GOVERNMENT OF INDIA 
MINISTRY OF CONSUMER AFFAIRS, FOOD AND PUBLIC DISTRIBUTION 
DEPARTMENT OF CONSUMER AFFAIRS 
 
RAJYA SABHA 
UNSTARRED QUESTION No. 1517 
TO BE ANSWERED ON 29.07.2022 
 
LEVYING OF SERVICE CHARGE BY RESTAURANTS 
 
 
1517.  SHRI NARANBHAI J. RATHWA 
 
Will the Minister of CONSUMER AFFAIRS, FOOD AND PUBLIC 
DISTRIBUTION be pleased to state: 
 
(a)  whether restaurant owners are levying service charge at the rate of 10 per cent on actual bills which 
is illegal and amounts to unfair trade practice; 
(b) if so, details thereof with total amount of service charge collected by these restaurant owners during 
last three years; 
(c)  whether Government has any plans to draw a legal framework on service charges being levied from 
consumers in restaurants and if so, details thereof; and 
(d) the precise reasons to levy service charge when the employees of restaurants are paid their 
wages/services/allowances, etc. and steps Government is contemplating to stop this unfair trade 
practice forthwith? 
 
 
ANSWER 
 
THE MINISTER OF STATE, CONSUMER AFFAIRS, FOOD AND PUBLIC 
DISTRIBUTION 
 
(SHRI ASHWINI KUMAR CHOUBEY) 
 
(a) to (d) :  A number of complaints were being received on the National Consumer Helpline regarding 
addition of service charge by restaurants and hotels in the bill without consent of consumers. Since a 
component of service is inherent in the price of food and beverages offered by the restaurant or hotel, and 
pricing of the product covers both the goods and services component, the Central Consumer Protection 
Authority, taking cognizance of such complaints being received on National Consumer Helpline, has issued 
the guidelines to prevent unfair trade practices and protection of consumer interest with regard to levy of 
service charge in hotels and restaurants on 4th July, 2022. The guidelines issued by the CCPA stipulate that 
hotels and restaurants shall not add service charge automatically or by default in the food bill and have to 
clearly inform the consumer that service charge is voluntary, optional and at consumer’s d iscretion. These 
guidelines do not prohibit a consumer to pay tips to serving staff voluntarily.   
There is no restriction on 
hotels or restaurants to set the prices at which they want to offer food or beverages to consumers. 
 
 
    Hon’ble High Court of Del hi has, vide its order dated 20.07.2022, stayed para 7 of these guidelines 
with further directions that the proposed levy of a service charge in addition to the price and taxes payable 
and the obligation of customers to pay the same shall be duly and prominently displayed on the menu or 
other places where it may deemed to be expedient; and that no service charge be levied on any take away 
items.

GOVERNMENT OF INDIA

UNSTARRED QUESTION NO.1519 
TO BE ANSWERED ON 29TH JULY, 2022 
RANKING OF STATES FOR IMPLEMENTATION OF NFSA  
1519  SHRI V. VIJAYASAI REDDY:  
Will the Minister of CONSUMER AFFAIRS, FOOD AND PUBLIC DISTRIBUTION 
be pleased to state: 
(
a) whether  it  is  a  fact  that  the  Ministry has ranked States on implementation of the
National Food Security Act (NFSA);
(b) whether  it  is  also  a  fact  that Andhra Pradesh is one of the top three States in the
implementation of NFSA;
(c) whether  does  it  not  mean that Andhra Pradesh is effectively implementing Targeted
Public Distribution System (TPDS) operations; and
(d) if so,  the  incentives/encouragement  that  the  Ministry  would  give  to  the performing
States like Andhra Pradesh?
A N S W E R 
MINISTER OF STATE FOR MINISTRY OF RURAL DEVELOPMENT AND 
CONSUMER AFFAIRS, FOOD & PUBLIC DISTRIBUTION 
(SADHVI NIRANJAN JYOTI) 
(
a):  Yes Sir, to evaluate the implementation of the National Food Security Act, 2013 
(NFSA) and Targeted Public Distribution System (TPDS) operations across the States and 
Union Territories, the Government has released first ever State Ranking Index for the NFSA 
on 5th of July, 2022. 
(b) & (c): Odisha, Uttar Pradesh and Andhra Pradesh scored first, second and third position 
respectively in the Index. The states performed very well on all the parameters and indicators 
governing the implementation of NFSA through TPDS.  
The Index rests upon three pillars that consider various facts of food security and 
nutrition. Each pillar has parameters and sub-parameters that support this evaluation. The first 
pillar measures coverage of NFSA, rightful targeting, and implementation of all provisions 
under NFSA. The second pillar analyses the delivery platform while considering the allocation 
of food grains, their movement, and last-mile delivery to Fair Price Shops. The third pillar 
focuses on nutrition initiatives of the Department.  
(d):  The State Ranking Index aims to create an environment of competition, cooperation, 
and learning among states, promote transparency and publish verified data in the public 
domain for research and analysis. At present, no proposal to incentivize the better performing 
States in the State Ranking is under consideration of the Government.

GOVERNMENT OF INDIA  
MINISTRY OF ELECTRONICS AND INFORMATION TECHNOLOGY 
RAJYA SABHA 
UNSTARRED QUESTION NO. 1526 
TO BE ANSWERED ON 29.07.2022 
 
 
LAWS TO MAKE SOCIAL MEDIA PLATFORMS ACCOUNTABLE 
  
1526.     SHRI K.C. VENUGOPAL:  
 
Will the Minister of Electronics and Information Technology be pleased to state: 
 
(a) whether Government is planning to bring in laws to make social media platforms more 
accountable;  
 
(b) if so, details thereof;  
 
(c) whether Government is planning to bring in new social media rules to give users a 
grievance appeal mechanism against arbitrary content moderation, inaction or takedown 
decisions of big tech companies;  
 
(d) if so, details thereof;  
 
(e) whether new IT intermediary rules, introduced last year that required social media 
platforms to remove any content flagged by authorities within stipulated timelines and set up 
robust complaint redressal mechanism with an officer being based in country, were put into 
effect; and  
 
(f) if so, details thereof? 
 
ANSWER 
 
MINISTER OF STATE FOR ELECTRONICS AND INFORMATION TECHNOLOGY 
 (SHRI RAJEEV CHANDRASEKHAR) 
  
(a) to (f):    Government’s objective is to ensure an Open, Safe & Trusted and Accountable 
Internet for its users. In line with the above objectives, the Government has notified the 
Information Technology (Intermediary Guidelines and Digital Media Ethics Codes) Rules, 
2021 (“IT Rules, 2021”) under the Information Technology Act, 2000. Government  has from 
time to time taken steps to address the evolving concerns and also to ensure that the 
intermediaries including the social media intermediaries remain accountable and transparent 
to the citizens for their services 
in India.   
  
As per the IT Rules, 2021, the online intermediaries including Social media intermediaries 
have been mandated to develop a robust grievance redressal system for their users. They are 
also expected to remove any unlawful content as and when brought to their knowledge either 
through a court order or through a notice by the appropriate government or its authorized 
agency. The said Rules also mandate the significant social media intermediaries (SSMI) to 
appoint a Resident Grievance Officer in India.

ANSWER 
 
MINISTER OF STATE FOR ELECTRONICS AND INFORMATION TECHNOLOGY 
(SHRI RAJEEV CHANDRASEKHAR) 
 
(a) and (b): Government of India’s goal is to make India a significant Electronics System 
Design and Manufacturing hub as part of its AtmaNirbhar Bharat economic policies. In this 
regard, Government has taken several strategic steps and initiatives for broadening and 
d
eepening our electronic manufacturing ecosystem. As a result of these initiatives, the 
production and export of electronic goods has grown at a CAGR of 17.9% and 16% 
respectively in the last five years. Also, India’s share in global electronics manufacturi ng
 has 
grown from 1.3% in 2012 to 8% in FY 21-22 and it contributed to about 4% of India’s total 
GDP in FY21-22. 
 
India has emerged as the 2nd largest manufacturer of mobile handsets in the world in volume 
terms. Over 200 units are manufacturing cellular mobile phones and parts / components 
thereof in the country, up from only 2 units in 2014.In volume terms, the production of 
Mobile Phones has gone up from about 6 crore mobile phones in 2014-15 to approximately 
32 
crore mobile phones in 2021-22. 
 
India is fast emerging as a trusted source/ County in the global supply chain for electronics. 
Electronics and IT is one of the emerging sectors for employment growth in India. The 
initiatives taken by the government has facilitated an eco-system for development of ESDM 
Sector in the entire country which has resulted in employment generation for over 57 lakh 
persons across the country as per industry estimates.

1528.    SMT. KANTA KARDAM: 
   
Will the Minister of ELECTRONICS AND INFORMATION TECHNOLOGY be pleased to

Outsourcing (BPO) across the country, especially in the State of Uttar Pradesh;  
 
(b)  if so, the details thereof; and  
 
(c) the steps taken by Government in the Information Technology/Information Technology 
Enabled Services (IT/ITeS) sector to generate employment opportunities in such States?  
 
ANSWER 
 
MINISTER OF STATE FOR  ELECTRONICS AND INFORMATION TECHNOLOGY 
(SHRI RAJEEV CHANDRASEKHAR)  
 
 
(a) and
 (b): Yes, Sir. To create employment opportunities and dispersal of IT/ITES industry in 
small cities and towns, Government had launched two BPO promotion schemes, namely India 
BPO Promotion Scheme (IBPS) and North East BPO Promotion Scheme (NEBPS) under Digital 
India Program. These schemes aim to incentivize setting up of BPO/ITES operations by providing 
50% financial support up to Rs. 1 lakh per seat in the form of viability gap funding. The duration 
of IBPS and NEBPS was upto 31.03.2019 and 31.03.2020 respectively to invite new bids, 
however disbursement to continue beyond this period as per scheme’s timelines. Since the 
inception of these schemes, 246 units have setup BPO/ITES operations across 27 States/UTs of 
the country, providing direct employment to 50,934 persons. Out of these, 12 units have setup 
operations in Uttar Pradesh providing direct employment to 874 persons.  
 
(c):  In addition to BPO Promotion Schemes, Government has launched several programs to 
promote Start-Ups and entrepreneurship such as Next Generation Incubation Scheme (NGIS), 
Technology Incubation and Development of Entrepreneurs (TIDE 2.0) Scheme and various 
domain specific Centres of Excellence (CoEs) which also support in generating employment 
opportunities.

GOVERNMENT OF INDIA  
MINISTRY OF ELECTRONICS AND INFORMATION TECHNOLOGY 
RAJYA SABHA 
UNSTARRED QUESTION NO. 1531 
TO BE ANSWERED ON: 29.07.2022 
 
PROTECTION OF PERSONAL DATA OF USERS MAKING ONLINE PAYMENTS  
  
1531.    SHRI DEREK O’ BRIEN:   
 
Will the Minister of ELECTRONICS AND INFORMATION TECHNOLOGY be pleased to state:  
 
(a) whether there are privacy safeguards in place to protect the personal data of users who use online 
payment services, the details thereof; 
(b) whether the authorities can ask for personal data of users who have donated to media houses, the details 
thereof; 
(c) how is it ensured that once the data is given to Government, it is not used for any other reason than it was 
taken for; 
(d) whether there are provisions for deletion of such data from Government servers after the purpose is 
served; and 
(e) if so, the details thereof, if not, the reasons therefor? 
 
 
ANSWER 
 
MINISTER OF STATE FOR ELECTRONICS AND INFORMATION TECHNOLOGY  
(SHRI RAJEEV CHANDRASEKHAR) 
 
(a): Yes, Sir. Privacy is a fundamental right as determined by Hon’ble Supreme Court in Justice K.S. 
Puttaswamy vs. Union of India in year 2017. This extends to digital informational privacy in the online 
space including but not limited to online payments.  
 
Further, The Central Government, in exercise of its powers under the Information Technology Act, 2000, 
has made the Information Technology (Reasonable Security Practices and Procedures and Sensitive Personal 
Data or Information) Rules, 2011 [“SPDI Rules”]. These require that a body corporate or any person 
collecting, receiving, possessing, storing, dealing or handling information provided observe various 
safeguards to protect the personal data of users. The safeguards include the requirements to provide and 
publish on the website of such body corporate or person a policy for privacy and disclosure of personal 
information   data or information, to use information collected for the purpose for which it has been 
collected, to keep it secure, to obtain prior permission of the information provider for disclosing   personal 
data. 
 
Also, the Reserve Bank of India, which is the designated authority for the regulation and supervision of 
payment systems under the Payment and Settlement Systems Act, 2007, has apprised that section 22 of the 
said Act provides for the duty of a payment service provider to keep documents in the payment system 
confidential.  
 
(b): Under rule 6 of the SPDI Rules, disclosure of sensitive personal data or information by a body corporate 
to any third party requires prior permission from the provider of such information. However, the rule further 
provides that information shall be shared, without obtaining prior consent from provider of information, with 
Government agencies mandated under the law to obtain information for the purpose of verification of 
identity, or for prevention, detection, investigation, prosecution and punishment of offences. 
(c) to (e): The National Data Governance Framework Policy is presently under consultation with 
stakeholders.

GOVERNMENT OF INDIA 
MINISTRY OF ELECTRONICS AND INFORMATION TECHNOLOGY 
RAJYA SABHA 
UNSTARRED QUESTION NO. 1533 
TO BE ANSWERED ON: 29.07.2022 
 
PROMOTION OF CHIP MANUFACTURING  
 
1533.  DR. ASHOK KUMAR MITTAL: 
 
Will the Minister of ELECTRONICS AND INFORMATION TECHNOLOGY be pleased to 
state: 
 
(a) the steps to be taken by Government to promote chip manufacturing in the country; 
 
(b) whether the country would be importing raw materials required for production of chips, as 
a large amount of the country’s total imports are electrical components and silicon wafers; 
and 
 
 
(c) how would the country reduce its absolute dependence on semiconductors from the 
foreign markets, as India imports 100 per cent of its semiconductors? 
 
 
ANSWER 
 
MINISTER OF STATE FOR ELECTRONICS AND INFORMATION TECHNOLOGY 
(SHRI RAJEEV CHANDRASEKHAR) 
 
(a) and (c) : Government is very focused on its important objective of building the overall 
semiconductor ecosystem and ensure that, it in- turn catalyses India’s rapidly expanding 
electronics manufacturing and innovation ecosystem. Government has approved the Semicon 
India programme with a total outlay of INR 76,000 crore for the development of 
semiconductor and display manufacturing ecosystem in the country. The programme aims to 
provide financial support to companies investing in semiconductors, display manufacturing 
and design ecosystem. 
 
Following four schemes are introduced under the aforesaid programme:  
 
i. Scheme for setting up of Semiconductor Fabs in India  
provides fiscal support to 
eligible applicants for setting up of Semiconductor Fabs which is aimed at attracting 
large investments for setting up semiconductor wafer fabrication facilities in the 
country.  
 
ii. Scheme for setting up of Display Fabs in India p
rovides fiscal support to eligible 
applicants for setting up of Display Fabs which is aimed at attracting large 
investments for setting up TFT LCD / AMOLED based display fabrication facilities 
in the country. 
 
iii. Scheme for setting up of Compound Semiconductors / Silicon Photonics / Sensors 
Fab and Semiconductor Assembly, Testing, Marking and Packaging (ATMP) / OSAT 
facilities in India.  
 
 
iv. Design Linked Incentive (DLI) Scheme 
offers financial incentives, design 
infrastructure support across various stages of development and deployment of 
semiconductor design for Integrated Circuits (ICs), Chipsets, System on Chips 
(SoCs), Systems & IP Cores and semiconductor linked design. 
 
174

In addition to the above schemes, Government has also approved modernisation of Semi-
Conductor Laboratory, Mohali as a brownfield Fab. 
 
(b) : Semiconductor manufacturing requires many high purity chemicals, bulk as well as 
speciality gases. Many chemicals and gasses required for semiconductor manufacturing are 
produced in India, which are currently used by different segments of the Indian industry. 
These chemicals and gasses can be upgraded to semiconductor grade by improving the 
quality and purity. Government, through schemes/ policies, is creating robust supply chain 
ecosystem for raw materials, chemicals and high purity gases for manufacturing of 
semiconductors in the country

GOVERNMENT OF INDIA 
MINISTRY OF FISHERIES, ANIMAL HUSBANDRY AND DAIRYING  
DEPARTMENT OF FISHERIES

UNSTARRED QUESTION No. 1534 
To be answered on 29th July, 2022 
 
Impact of IUU fishing in India 
1534.  DR. K. LAXMAN 
     Will the Minister of Fisheries, Animal Husbandry and Dairying be pleased to state: 
(a) whether there is any parameter to assess the Illegal, Unreported and Unregulated (IUU)      
fishing in India;  
(b)  if so, the details thereof;  
(c)  the impact of IUU fishing on Indian economy; and  
(d)  the steps taken for protecting the interest of farmers, MSMEs and fisherman under the   
‘Geneva Package’ and how it will be helpful for India?  
 
ANSWER 
THE MINISTER OF FISHERIES, ANIMAL HUSBANDRY AND DAIRYING 
  (SHRI PARSHOTTAM RUPALA) 
(a) to (c): Illegal, Unreported and Unregulated Fishing (IUU Fishing) ‘inter alia’ means fishing 
activities undertaken by fishing vessels or operators without permission or in contravention of  the 
laws and regulations of the country or in contravention of the conservation and management measures 
of the relevant regional fisheries management organization to which a country is a party, or fishing 
activities not reported or fishing activities in unregulated areas of high seas. Countries determine IUU 
fishing as per their national framework of laws, regulations and/or administrative procedures. While 
there is no specific report available on the impact of IUU fishing on Indian economy, the overall 
economic loss resulting from illegal catches is estimated at USD 26 billion to USD 50 billion globally, 
as per independent researchers. 
(d):   India played a leadership role in bridging the differences and bringing together the WTO 
members on various multilateral issues in WTO including the Agreement on Fisheries Subsidies  
during the 12th Ministerial Conference of WTO held in Geneva from 12th June 2022 to 17th June 2022. 
The Agreement on Fisheries Subsidies was adopted on 17thJune 2022.  During the negotiations India 
ensured that the long term interests of the traditional and small -scale fishers of developing countries 
including India are adequately protected, by ensuring that there is no restriction of subsidies given to 
fishermen for fishing in the Territorial Waters, Exclusive Economic Zone (EEZ) and in the areas of 
high seas within the Regional Fisheries Management Organizations (RFMOs)  except for IUU and 
overfished categories. Thus the subsidies provided to the fishermen under various central and State  
176

 
 
 
 
Government schemes and programs will be continued as usual. As per the Agreement, subsidies given 
by advanced fishing countries to their industrial fishing vessels for fishing in unregulated high seas 
and doing IUU fishing will be stopped henceforth. It is expected that this will reduce industrial fishing 
and make more fish available to our fishermen. With the implementation of the Agreement, India will 
be in a position to act against subsidies provided to foreign vessels operating adjacent to Indian EEZ. 
Further, on India’s insistence the Agreement provides for determination of IUU fishing or 
overfished stocks to be done by the coastal nation for fishing in its Territorial waters and EEZ. Thus 
no outside interference in this regard is expected.

1535  SHRI SHAKTISINH GOHIL: 
Will the Minister of Fisheries, Animal Husbandry and Dairying  
be pleased to state 
 
(a) in which States, State Animal Welfare Boards have not been established; 
(b) the reasons for the same; and 
(c ) the action taken against responsible authorities? 
 
ANSWER 
THE MINISTER OF FISHERIES ANIMAL HUSBANDRY AND DAIRYING 
(SHRI PARSHOTTAM RUPALA) 
(a) The Union Territories of Ladakh and Lakshadweep have not established State Animal 
Welfare Board. 
(b)  The Union Territories of Ladakh has been newly formed and being a Small Union Territories 
of Lakshadweep, these two Union Territories have yet to establish the State Animal Welfare 
Board. Further, there is no provision for establishment of State Animal Welfare Board in the 
existing Prevention of Cruelty to Animal Act, 1960. The States are establishing the State Animal 
Welfare Board base d on the Hon’ble Supreme Court d irection and request of the Central 
Government.  
(c) Central Government and Animal Welfare Board of India issued several circulars/advisories to 
the State Governments/Union Territories for establishment of State Animal Welfare Board.

( c)  the action taken against such meat/poultry shops and how many such shops were closed?

THE MINISTER FOR FISHERIES ANIMAL HUSBANDRY AND DAIRYING 
(SHRI PARSHOTTAM RUPALA) 
(a) As per the information received from the Food Safety Standards Authority of India ,  number 
of registered meat/poultry shops State-wise as on 31.03.2022 is at Annexure I 
(b) The information is not maintained by the Central Government. 
(c) Food Safety and Standards Authority of India (FSSAI) has laid down the standards for meat 
and meat products under Regulation No. 2.5 of Food Safety and Standards (Food Products 
Standards & Food Additives) Regulatio ns, 2011, which are required to be complied by Food 
Business Operators (FBOs), engaged in Meat and Meat Products.  
In addition, FSSAI has prescribed guidelines on Specific Hygienic and Sanitary practices to be 
followed by FBOs engaged in manufacture, proce ssing, storing and selling of Meat and Meat 
Products.   
The implementation and enforcement of Food Safety and Standards Act, 2006 primarily rests 
with the State/ UT Governments. 
Random sampling and testing of food products including meat and meat products are being done 
by the officials of Food Safety Departments of the respective States/ UTs to check compliance of 
the standards laid down under the Food Safety and Standards Act, 2006, Rules and Regulations 
made thereunder. In cases where the food samples are found to be non-conforming, recourse is 
taken to penal provisions under Chapter IX of the FSS Act, 2006. The total number of 
Inspections done State-wise for compliance of the prescribed norms and standards for the year 
2020
-2021 is   at Annexure-II.

GAUSHALAS 
1538: SHRI DEEPAK PRAKASH: 
Will the Minister of Fisheries, Animal Husbandry and Dairying 
 be pleased to state 
(a) the number of Gaushalas (cowsheds) dedicated to the care of abandoned cows across the 
country including the State of Jharkhand; and 
(b) whether any efforts are being made by Government to identify the institutions running 
Gaushalas and provide financial assistance to them or strengthen such institutions, in the future? 
 
ANSWER 
THE MINISTER OF FISHERIES ANIMAL HUSBANDRY AND DAIRYING 
(SHRI PARSHOTTAM RUPALA) 
 (a) The Gaushalas  are registered by the respective State Governments , hence, no information is 
maintained.  However, the Animal Welfare Board of India (AWBI) recognizes Animal Welfare 
Organization including Gaushala those are applied  for recognisition  in the Board . These 
organizations are working for the care of abandoned/domestic animals. As on date the AWBI has 
recognized 10 Gaushalas in the State of Jharkhand. 
(b) The recognized organizations are providing shelter to the Stray / Homeless animals including 
cow, dog etc., rescue animals from illegal transportation and other unforeseen circumstances, 
providing medical treatment and care, conducting animal birth control programs, providing 
ambulance services, conducting awareness and training programs and other activities to prevent the 
unnecessary pain and suffering to animals.  
 
 To encourage their work, the Animal Welfare Board of India is providing grants for feeding of 
animals and veterinary care under Regular Grants, providing grants for Shelters of animals, 
providing ambulance under the Ambulance Grants. Further, many State Governments are also 
providing assistance to the gaushalas.

Production and export of fish and fishery products 
 
1539. SHRI JOSE K MANI: 
Will the Minister of Fisheries, Animal Husbandry and Dairying be pleased to state: 
(a)  whether Government has the data regarding the overall production of fisheries sector in  
the last two years; 
(b)  If so, the details thereof; 
(c)  whether Government has noticed any improvement in the production of the fisheries sector 
since the implementation of Pradhan Mantri Matsya Sampada Yojana (PMMSY); and 
(d)  whether Government has taken any steps to promote the export of fish and fishery products 
which has shown a decline in the fiscal year 2021? 
 
ANSWER 
    THE MINISTER FOR FISHERIES, ANIMAL HUSBANDRYAND DAIRYING 
(SHRI PARSHOTTAM RUPALA) 
(a) and (b): The overall fish production recorded in the country during the last two years is as 
below:  
Year Fish Production (in lakh tonnes) 
2020-21 147.25 
2021-22 ( Provisional) 161.87 
 
(c): PMMSY is being implemented from FY 2020-21 for a period of 5 years till 2024-25. The fish 
production during the implementation of Pradhan Mantri Matsya Sampada Yojana (PMMSY) has 
shown an increasing trend and it has increased from 141.64 Lakh tonne in 2019-20 to 161.87 Lakh 
tonne in 2021-22 (P). 
(d): There was a fall in exports due to global impact of Covid-19 pandemic during 2020-
21.
However, the trend was reversed since then and during the year 2021-22, the export of fish and 
fishery products stood at an all- time high of Rs. 57,586.48 Crore (provisional) as against Rs. 
43,720.98 Crore in 2020-21.  
 
184

 
 
Pradhan Mantri Matsya Sampada Yojana (PMMSY) implemented by the Department of 
Fisheries, Ministry of Fisheries, Animal Husbandry inter-alia envisages support for India’s export 
competitiveness and higher price realization. These include quality fish production, expansion, 
diversification and intensification of brackish water aquaculture, promotion of export-oriented 
species, cold chain infrastructure facilities including cold storages, ice plants, preprocessing units, 
infusion of technology, robust disease management framework, promotion of good aquaculture 
practices, branding, standards, certification and traceability, training and capacity building, 
creation of modern post-harvest infrastructure, development of modern fishing harbours and fish 
landing centres, etc. Further,
 a dedicated fund namely Fisheries and Aquaculture Infrastructure 
Development Fund (FIDF) with a total fund size of Rs. 7522.48 crore has been created to provide 
concessional finance to eligible entities including State Government/ Union Territories and private 
entrepreneurs for creation and strengthening of fisheries infrastructure that among other things, 
will improve export competitiveness.

NATIONAL KAMDHENU BREEDING CENTER 
 
1540  SHRI RAJMANI PATEL: 
 
Will the Minister of FISHERIES, ANIMAL HUSBANDRY AND DAIRYING  
 
be pleased to state: 
 
(a) whether it is a fact that the National Kamdhenu Breeding Center (NKBC) scheme is being 
run by Government:  
(b) the number of centers set up so far under the NKBC scheme and the State/UT-wise and 
district-wise number of breeds being conserved and preserved in the country including the 
State of Madhya Pradesh; and 
(c) whether the conservation and preservation centers under the said scheme is operational in 
district Rewa, Madhya Pradesh, if not, by when it will be made operational, the details 
thereof?  
ANSWER 
THE MINISTER OF FISHERIES, ANIMAL HUSBANDRY AND DAIRYING 
 
(SHRI PARSHOTTAM RUPALA) 
(a) and (b)  The Department of Animal Husbandry and Dairying has established two 
National Kamdhenu Breeding Centers as repository of germplasm of Indigenous Bovine 
Breeds and to take up development and conservation of indigenous breeds in scientific and 
holistic manner  under the Centrally Sponsored scheme Rashtriya Gokul Mission. The 
Northern Region National Kamdhenu Breeding Center has been established at Kiratpur, 
Itarsi, in Madhya Pradesh and at present centre has been maintaining 14
 breeds of cattle and 
5 breeds of buffalo.  The Southern Region National Kamdhenu Breeding Center has been 
established at Chintaladevi, Nellore, in Andhra Pradesh and at present centre has been 
maintaining 13 breeds of cattle and 4 breeds of Buffalo.   
 
(c) As per the revised and realigned guidelines of Rashtriya Gokul Mission 2021 -2026, 
establishment of conservation and preservation centers is not an admissible component. 
However, any private individual entrepreneurs, FPOs, SHGs, FCOs, JLGs, and Section 8 
companies can apply for establishment of Breed Multiplication farm with a capacity of 200 
cows or buffaloes preferably of indigenous breeds and capital subsidy of Rs.2.00 crore is 
made available under the component. Further, applicant can also avail 3% interest 
subvention for Breed Multiplication farms under the Animal Husbandry Infrastructure 
Development Fund.

UNNAT PASHUDHAN SASHAKT KISAN CONCLAVE 
 
1541.  SHRI SANJAY RAUT: 
 
Will the Minister of Fisheries, Animal Husbandry and Dairying 
 be pleased to state: 
 
(a) whether the Government has organized Unnat Pashudhan Sashakt Kisan Conclave recently and if 
so, the details thereof; 
(b) whether the Government has taken any step to provide quality animal health services at the 
farmers’ doorsteps and if so, the details thereof; 
(c) whether Government proposes to provide more importance to animal husbandry, dairy and 
fisheries sectors and if so, the steps proposed to be taken by the Government in this regard; and 
(d) the other steps taken by the Government to ensure growth of the livestock sector by increasing 
farmers’ access to animal health and credit services? 
 
 
ANSWER 
THE MINISTER OF FISHERIES, ANIMAL HUSBANDRY AND DAIRYING  
(SHRI PARSHOTTAM RUPALA) 
 
(a) As a part of Azadi Ka Amrit Mahotsav, Department of Animal Husbandry & Dairying, Ministry of 
Fisheries, Animal Husbandry and Dairying organised Unnat Pashudhan Sashakt Kisan Conclave on 1st 
June, 2022 with a focus on dairy, poultry and other livestock farmers, innovative entrepreneurs, start-
ups and industry on the occasion of World Milk Day. Following programmes were organized during the 
Conclave: 
(i) Digital Exhibition of 75 Indigenous Livestock Breeds. 
(ii) Digital Exhibition of 75 successful Entrepreneurs in livestock sector. 
(iii) Launch of two digital coffee table books on 75 Livestock breeds and 75 Entrepreneurs. 
(iv) Launch of training programme for Accredited Agent for Health and Extension of Livestock 
Production (A-HELP). 
(v) Felicitation of Padmashree awardees from the Animal Husbandry sector. 
(vi) Announcement of Animal Husbandry Startup Grand challenge 2.0 winners. 
(vii) Three technical thematic sessions on 1) Increasing productivity and improving animal health , 2) 
Value addition and market linkages 
and 3) Innovation and technology.  
 
(b) In order  to provide quality animal health services at the farmer's doorstep, Government has 
implemented  "Establishment & Strengthening of Veterinary Hospitals & Dispensaries - Mobile 
Veterinary Units (ESVHD -MVU)"  under “Livestock Health & Disease Control   Scheme”  to provide 
veterinary health services at farmer's doorstep from   2021-22.  The scheme provides 100% financial 
assistance to al l States/UTs towards non-recurring expenditure on procurement of  Mobile Veterinary 
Unit (MVU) for every one lakh livestock population. For  recurring expenditure on running of 
187

diagnostic & treatment services by these Mobile Veterinary Units, the scheme provides a central share 
of 90% for North Eastern States & Himalayan States, 100% for UTs and 60% for all other 
States. During 2021-22 a sum of Rs.681.57 Crore has been released to 33 States/UTs for procurement of 
4332 Mobile Veterinary Units (MVUs). 
 
(c) Recently, the Government has revised and realigned various components of existing schemes in 
order to have focused development of animal husbandry and dairying across the country for next 5 
years starting from 2021-22. Ministry of Fisheries, Animal Husbandry and Dairying is implementing 
the following schemes to supplement the efforts of the States/UTs across the country: 
 
i. Rashtriya Gokul Mission,  
ii. National Programme for Dairy Development,  
iii. Dairy Processing and Infrastructure Development Fund,  
iv. Supporting Dairy Cooperatives and Farmer Producer Organizations engaged in dairy activities 
v. National Livestock Mission,  
vi. Livestock Health and Disease Control 
vii. Animal Husbandry Infrastructure Development Fund 
viii. National Animal Disease Control Programme 
ix. Pradhan Mantri Matsya Sampada Yojana 
x. Fisheries and Aquaculture Infrastructure Development Fund 
 
(d) In order to ensure growth of the livestock sector, National Animal Disease Control Programme 
(NADCP) is being implemented for control and eradication of Foot & Mouth Disease (FMD) and 
Brucellosis disease in the country with 100 % central support to States by vaccinating against FMD and 
Brucellosis. Under Livestock Health & Disease Control (LH & DC) scheme, the component 
"Assistance to States for Control of Animal Diseases (ASCAD)" provides central assistance (90% for 
North Eastern States & Himalayan States, 100% for UTs and 60% for all other States) to all States/UTs 
towards vaccination of economically important diseases, strengthening of disease diagnostic 
laboratories. The scheme also includes Peste des Petits Ruminant - Eradication Programme (PPR-EP)  
and Classical Swine Fever Control Programme (CSF-CP) 
, which provides  100% central assistance  to 
all States/UTs  for eradication of Peste des Petits Ruminant (PPR) disease of sheep & goats  and  control 
of  CSF disease of pigs  by vaccination coverage of 100% eligible sheep & goat  and pig population in 
the country. 
 In order to increase the access to credit services to Animal husbandry and Dairy Sector, the 
Department is implementing Credit Linked Infrastructure Development Schemes such as Dairy 
Processing Infrastructure Development Fund (DIDF), Animal Husbandry Infrastructure Development 
Fund(AHIDF), Supporting Dairy Cooperatives and Farmer producer organizations involved in dairy 
activities (SDCFPO) and credit linked Entrepreneurship Schemes such as Breed Multiplication Farm 
under Rashtriya Gokul Mission(RGM), Sheep, goat , Pig breeder farm & breed development in rural 
poultry under National Livestock Mission. Special drives were carried out in association with D/o 
Financial Services (DFS) to provide fresh KCC to animal husbandry and dairy farmers in the country. 
The Department in association with D/o Financial Services is currently organizing a Nation-wide 
AHDF KCC campaign from 15.11.2021 to 31.07.2022. In order to ensure increased credit flow, a target 
of Rs 1,04,580 Crore is fixed as ground level credit target for Animal Husbandry and Dairying during 
2022-23.

CENTRAL SECTOR UMBRELLA SCHEME SAMPADA 
 
  
1544. SHRI MUZIBULLA KHAN: 
   SHRI PRAMOD TIWARI: 
  
Will the Minister of FOOD PROCESSING INDUSTRIES be pleased to state: 
 
  
(a) the details of fund allocated and released under Central Sector Umbrella Scheme - 
Pradhan Mantri Kisan SAMPADA Yojana (PMKSY); 
  
(b) the details of the investment made by Government under scheme for Agro-Marine 
Processing and Development of Agro-Processing Cluster (SAMPADA); 
  
(c) whether 'SAMPADA' scheme has benefitted farmers and/or generated employment  in 
agriculture sector; 
  
(d) if so, the details thereof; 
  
(e) if not, the reason therefor; and  
  
(f) the steps being taken by Government to increase processing level and enhance  export 
of processed foods? 
  
ANSWER 
  
THE MINISTER OF STATE FOR FOOD PROCESSING INDUSTRIES 
(SHRI PRAHLAD SINGH PATEL) 
  
(a) to (f): The Ministry has been implementing the Scheme, ‘Pradhan Mantri Kisan Sampada 
Yojana (PMKSY)’, a comprehensive package for creation of modern infrastructure with 
efficient supply chain management from farm gate to retail outlet. It not only provides boost to 
the growth of food processing sector in the country but also helps in providing better returns to 
farmers, creating employment opportunities, reducing wastage of agricultural produce, 
increasing the processing level and enhancing the export of the processed foods. 
  
SAMPADA is a digital platform through which various projects sanctioned under 
different sub-schemes of PMKSY is monitored. 
  
            A total 853 projects for setting up food processing units, have been sanctioned so far by 
the Ministry und er different sub -schemes of PMKSY with total  project cost of Rs.21058.29 
crore and approved grants-in-aid of Rs.6673.74 crore, out of which Rs.4444.25 crore has been 
released. These projects entail processing and preservation capacity of 216.81 lakh MT per  
annum and 70.014 lakh MT per annum respectively, benefiting 41,42,917 farmers and 
generating 10,61,361 employment.

 MINISTRY OF FOOD PROCESSING INDUSTRIES 
RAJYA SABHA 
 UNSTARRED QUESTION NO- 1545 
 ANSWERED ON- 29.07.2022 
  
  
NIFTEMs FUNCTIONING IN THE COUNTRY 
  
1545. SHRI SANJAY RAUT: 
   
Will the Minister of Food Processing Industries be pleased to state: 
  
(a) The National Institutes of Food Technology Entrepreneurship and Management 
(NIFTEMs) functioning in the country;  
 
(b) The courses being offered in the field of food technology and allied sectors; 
  
(c) Whether NIFTEMs have been able to meet the increasing demand of manpower in the 
FPIs, if so, the details thereof, if not, corrective steps taken in this regard; 
  
(d) Whether the Government has assessed and reviewed the functioning of the NIFTEMs, 
if so, details of assessment along with the corrective measures taken on the shortcomings; and 
  
(e) The targets set for creation of employment opportunities in the food processing sector 
in the next five years?   
  
ANSWER 
  
THE MINISTER OF STATE FOR FOOD PROCESSING INDUSTRIES 
(SHRI PRAHLAD SINGH PATEL) 
  
(a): Sir, presently there are two National Institutes of Food Technology  Entrepreneurship 
and Management (NIFTEM), functioning in our country. One in Kundli, Haryana and the other 
at Thanjavur, Tamil Nadu.  
  
(b): The courses offered by both the institutes are; B.Tech, M.Tech and Ph.D. in  Food 
Technology and allied sectors.  MBA course is offered by NIFTEM Kundli.  
  
(c): To meet the increasing demand of manpower, the sanctioned intake of the students have 
been incr eased. There are other Centrally funded Institutions/ Universities, State run 
Institutions/ Universities and private Institutions/ Universities offering degree, post graduate 
degree and doctorate degree in food technology and its allied specialization to meet increasing 
demand of manpower in the Food Processing Industries. 
  
(d): A Review Committee was set up by Ministry of Food Processing Industries(MoFPI) to 
review the activities of both the NIFTEMs. The Review Committee reported that both the 
Institutes have been doing very well ever since their inception with respect to the mandated 
objectives. New vistas in the contemporary areas of food science and technology for the 
institute to catch up with other similar institutes of international repute have  also been started.  
  
(e): The MoFPI facilitates generation of employment in the food processing sector through 
Schemes Pradhan Mantri Kisan Sampada Yojana (PMKSY), Pradhan Mantri Formalisation of 
Micro Food Processing Enterprises (PMFME) and Production -Linked Incentive Scheme 
(PLIS). No specific target has been set for the next five years.

GOVERNMENT OF INDIA 
MINISTRY OF FOOD PROCESSING INDUSTRIES 
RAJYA SABHA 
UNSTARRED QUESTION NO- 1546 
ANSWERED ON- 29.07.2022 
  
SPECIAL SCHEME TO SET UP FPIs 
   
 
1546. SMT. GEETA ALIAS CHANDRAPRABHA: 
  
Will the Minister of Food Processing Industries be pleased to state: 
 
  
(a) whether any special scheme is being run to set up Food Processing Industries(FPIs) in 
the country; 
 
(b) if so, the types of food processing industries covered under this scheme; 
 
(c) whether any financial assistance is being provided to setup these industries; and 
 
(d) the total number of FPIs set up in Uttar Pradesh during last 2 years, the details thereof? 
  
  
ANSWER 
THE MINISTER OF STATE FOR FOOD PROCESSING INDUSTRIES  
(SHRI PRAHLAD SINGH PATEL) 
  
(a) to (d):  Yes sir, Ministry of Food Processing Industries (MoFPI) incentivizes setting up/ 
expansion of Food Processing Industries in the country through its Central Sector Umbrella 
Scheme Pradhan Mantri Kisan SAMPADA Yojana (PMKSY), Production Linked Incentive 
Scheme for Food Processing Industry (PLISFPI) and Centrally sponsored PM Formalization of 
Micro Food Processing Enterprises (PMFME) scheme. These schemes cover entire food 
processing value chain from farm gate to retail outlet and Agro - horticulture, marine, meat etc 
based Food Processing Industries are assisted through them. 
 
               Under component schemes of PMKSY, MoFPI mostly provides credit linked financial 
assistance (capital subsidy) in the form of grants -in-aid to entrepreneurs, which varies from 
35% of the eligible project cost in general areas to 50% in hilly & difficult areas. The details of 
Food Processing Industries set up in Uttar Pradesh during last 2 years under component 
schemes of PMKSY is placed at Annexure. 
 
               MoFPI provides  credit linked grants -in-aid @35% of eligible project cost with 
maximum of Rs. 10.00 lakhs to micro food processing units through PMFME scheme. So far, 
a total of 344 Loans have been sanctioned under the scheme in Uttar Pradesh.

GOVERNMENT OF INDIA 
MINISTRY OF HEAVY INDUSTRIES 
RAJYA SABHA 
UNSTARRED QUESTION NO. 1548 
ANSWERED ON 29.07.2022 
EXPANDING THE SCOPE OF SAMARTH UDYOG BHARAT 4.0 SCHEME 
1548. SHRI P. WILSON:  
Will the Minister of Heavy Industries be pleased to state: 
(a) whether the Ministry is considering expanding the scope of SAMARTH Udyog Bharat 4.0
scheme as there are only five Common Engineering Facility Centre Projects currently under the
scheme;
(b) how many incubators has the Ministry recognised under the scheme; and
(c) how many partners has the Ministry collaborated with in order to  achieve the objectives of
the scheme and if so, the details thereof and if there are no partners so far, the reasons therefor?
ANSWER 
THE MINISTER OF STATE FOR HEAVY INDUSTRIES 
(SHRI KRISHAN PAL GURJAR) 
(a) Under the Scheme for “Enhancement of Competitiveness in the Indian Capital Goods Sector”,
launched by Government in the year 2014, fifteen Common Engineering Facility Centres  (CEFCs)
including four Industry 4.0 SAMARTH centres  and 6 technology innovation platforms have been
established at IIT, Indian Institute of Science, Bengaluru, CMTI Bengaluru, Heavy Engineering
Corporation (HEC), Ranchi, HMT Bengaluru etc. to provide infrastructural and technical support to
Industrial clus ters including testing, training, certification, common manufacturing, tool room,
calibration to MSMEs.
In addition to this, the Phase 2 of the Scheme for Enhancement of Competitiveness in the 
Indian Capital Goods Sector has also been notified on 25th January, 2022.  
Under this Scheme, there is a component for “ Setting up of four Common Engineering 
Facility Centres (CEFCs) and augmentation of existing CEFCs”.  
Under this component, a project namely Common Engineering Facility Centre by BHEL 
for skilling in Advanced Welding Technologies has already been sanctioned with total project cost 
of Rs 87.06 crores. 
(b) No incubator has been identified under the Scheme so far.
(c) Under the Phase 1 of the Scheme, 4 SAMARTH Udyog Centre have been established in
collaboration with the following Academic and Research  Institutes/ Industries:
i. Central Manufacturing Technology Institute (CMTI), Bengaluru;
ii. IITD-AIA Foundation for Smart Manufacturing (IAFSM);
iii. Samarth Udyog Technology Forum (SUTF), Pune;
iv. Indian Institute of Science (IISc), Bengaluru

197

STATEMENT REFERRED TO IN REPLY TO PARTS (a) TO (d) OF UNSTARRED QUESTION 
NO. 1550 BY SHRI SAMIR ORAON  ANSWERED IN RAJYA SABHA ON 29.07.2022  
REGARDING  RANCHI-PATNA RAILWAY LINE 
(a) to (d):  No, Sir. At present, there is no proposal for construction of Railway line direct from 
Ranchi to Patna. Koderma is already connected to Patna by rail network and Railways have taken up 
a project namely Koderma-Ranchi (202 Km) new line project at a cost of ₹3,800 crore. An 
expenditure of ₹3,704 crore has been incurred on the project upto March, 2022 and 175 Km length 
has been completed. Work on remaining Sidhwar – Shanki (27 Km) section has been taken up. 
Outlay of ₹55 crore has also been provided for the Financial Year 2022-23. 
 
Since 2014, there has been substantial increase in Railway Budget allocation and commensurate 
commissioning. Average annual Budget allocation for infrastructure and safety works, falling 
fully/partly in the State of Jharkhand during 2014-19, has been enhan ced to ₹2,089 crore per year 
from ₹457 crore per year during 2009-14, which is 357% more than average annual budget outlay of 
2009-14. Budget outlay for these projects has been increased to ₹2,493 crore for financial year 2019-
20 (446% more than average annual outlay of 2009- 14), ₹3,221 crore for financial year 2020 -21 
(605% more than average annual budget outlay of 2009- 14) and ₹4,182 crore for financial year 
2021-22 (815% more than average annual budget outlay of 2009-14). For financial year 2022-23, 
highest-ever budget outlay of ₹5,058 crore has been provided for these projects, which is 1007% 
more than average annual budget outlay of 2009-14. 
 
During 2014-22, 911 Km sections (433 Km New Lines and 478 Km Doubling) falling fully/partly in 
the State of Jharkhand have been commissioned at an average rate of 113.88 Km/year, which is 
98.4% more than the average annual commissioning during 2009-14 (57.4 Km/year). 
 
Completion of any Railway project/s depends on various factors like quick land acquisition by State 
Government, forestry clearance by officials of forest department, deposition of State share by the 
State Government in cost sharing projects, shifting of infringing utilities, statutory clearances from 
various authorities, geological and topographical conditions of area, law and order situation in the 
area of project/s site, number of working months in a year for particular project site due to climatic 
conditions, etc and all these factors affect the completion time of the project/s. With above 
constraints, every effort is being made to complete these projects at the earliest.

199

 
 
 
STATEMENT REFERRED TO IN REPLY TO PARTS (a) TO (d) OF UNSTARRED QUESTION 
NO. 1551 BY SMT. JEBI MATHER HISHAM ANSWERED IN RAJYA SABHA ON 29.07.2022 
REGARDING ANGAMALI-ERUMELY SABARI RAILWAY PROJECT 
 
 
(a)  to (d): Angamali-Sabarimala new rail line was sanctioned in 1997 -98 at a cost of `550 crore. 
Total length of the project is 116 Km.  
  
5 km section of this project was passing through Periyar Tiger Reserve Forest. On the request 
of State Government, the project was terminated at Erumeli due to reserve forest area. Alignment of 
the project passes through three Districts i.e. Ernakulam, Idukki and Kottayam districts. Final 
location survey (FLS) for Angamali-Kaladi-Ramapuram section (70 Km) was completed in 2002 
and further survey in the Kottayam district was stopped by local people in 2007 due to objections on 
alignment. The final project report can be prepared only after Final Location Survey is completed. 
 
Consequently, in this project, work on Angamali-Kaladi (7 Km) and long lead works on 
Kaladi-Perumbavoor (10 Km) have been taken up. However, further works on this project could not 
be taken forward due to protests by local people against land acquisition and fixing of alignment of 
the line, court cases filed against the project and inadequate response from the State Government of 
Kerala.  
 
Government of Kerala vide their letter dated 27.11.2015 agreed to share 50% cost of the 
project. Government of Kerala had also identified this project for execution under Joint Venture 
Scheme with Ministry of Railways and therefore, an MoU was also signed on 01.09.2016.  
Unfortunately, within a year, the State Government vide letter dated 15.11.2016 withdrew their own 
consent to share 50% cost of the project.  Government Kerala has not deposited any cost share for 
the project. Project is held up on this account.  
 
After long persuasion, recently Government of Kerala vide letter dated 07.01.2021 has 
conveyed their decision to share 50% of the total project cost  of Angamali - Sabarimala Railway 
project through Kerala Infrastructure Investment Fund Board (KIIFB), subject to the condition that 
operation  and  maintenance  of  the  track  shall  be  done by Ministry of Railways while the stations  
 
…2/- 
 
 
 
200

 
 
-2- 
 
along the route  will be developed by a Special Purpose Vehicle (SPV) (through public private 
partnership route) and the net revenue realized by the SPV will be shared on a 50:50 basis between 
State Government and Ministry of Railways, after offsetting the cost of Operation & Maintenance 
(O&M) as agreed to by Railways. M/S KRDCL was advised to complete Final Location Survey and 
to update the project cost.   
  
 
 Accordingly, M/S KRDCL completed the Final Location Survey (FLS) and submitted the 
Detailed Project Report (DPR) and Detailed Estimate (DE) of the full project to Southern Railways 
on 23.06.2022. The DPR and DE is under examination. Updated detailed estimate submitted by M/s 
KRDCL is approx ` 3448 crore.

GOVERNMENT OF INDIA 
MINISTRY OF RAILWAYS 
 
RAJYA SABHA 
UNSTARRED QUESTION NO. 1552 
ANSWERED ON 29.07.2022 
 
DROP BOX FOR EMERGENCY QUOTA REQUISITION LETTERS  
 
1552# SMT. SEEMA DWIVEDI: 
 Will the Minister of RAILWAYS be pleased to state: 
 
(a) whether Government would consider  making arrangements for placing a permanent 
box in the “Rail Bhavan” premises under the surveillance of security personnel, in which 
the emergency quota requisition letters by Members of Parliament can be easily dropped 
any time;  
 
(b) whether Government is considering launching an authorised NIC e -mail id to which 
the Members of Parliament can send the emergency quota requisitions from their 
authorised NIC e-mail id; and 
 
(c) if so, by when this arrangement will be brought into force by Government? 
 
 
ANSWER 
 
MINISTER OF RAILWAYS, COMMUNICATIONS AND ELECTRONICS & 
INFORMATION TECHNOLOGY 
 
 
(SHRI 
ASHWINI VAISHNAW) 
 
(a) :  A box is placed outside Rail Bhawan to facilitate any person including general public 
to put request for release of berths out of Emergency Quota during office hours.  In 
addition, this box is also placed on Saturdays, Sundays and other holidays upto 1400 hrs.  
Further, during Parliament Session, to facilitate Members of Parliament attending the 
House, the staff attached with the office of Minister for Railways sitting in Parliament 
House receives the requests of Hon’ble Members of Parliament and sends it to Rail 
Bhawan for further necessary action. As it will not be feasible to clear this box after office 
hours, no useful purpose will be served by placing this box permanently.  As such, existing 
arrangements are considered adequate. 
(b) & (c) : With a view to avoid misuse of facility of release of accommodation out of 
Emergency Quota by unscrupulous elements, the emergency quota is released based on 
written requests received from officers authorized on this behalf. At present, there is no 
proposal under consideration to accept such requests through e-mail.

STATEMENT REFERRED TO IN REPLY TO PARTS (a) TO (g ) OF UNSTARRED 
QUESTION NO 1553 BY SHRI JOHN BRITTAS ANSWERED IN RAJYA SABHA ON 
29.07.2022 REGARDING STAFF STRENGTH AND VACANCIES IN RAILWAYS  
(a)       The total number of regular employees currently working in the Railways is 12,52,347 
as on 31.03.2021. 
(b) Total number of contractual employees appointed against non-gazetted posts in 
Railways as on 31.12.2021 is 5,079. 
(c)  As per Indian Railway Shramik Kalyan Portal, total number of 5,87,968 contract 
workers are registered as on 22.07.2022. 
(d) & (e) Contractors being the employers of contract labourers are required to abide by 
the provisions of the Minimum Wages Act,1948. Contract workers are paid by the contractors 
according to instructions issued by Ministry of Labour & Employment. These instructions are 
being circulated to Railways/PUs etc. for compliance. Contractual Paramedical staff are paid at 
par with regular employees. 
(f)      No post has been abolished in Railways from 2017 till date. 
(g) Non- gazetted Group’C’ (including level-1) vacancies on Railways as on 01.04.2022 are 
3,03,862(provisional) and in Gazetted cadre, there are 2473 vacancies. Vacancy position 
category-wise (SC/ST/OBC) is not maintained.

      GOVERNMENT OF INDIA 
MINISTRY OF RAILWAYS 
 
RAJYA SABHA 
UNSTARRED QUESTION NO. 1554 
ANSWERED ON 29.07.2022 
 
 
 
HIKE IN PASSENGER TRAIN FARE 
 
 
 
  1554. DR. AMEE YAJNIK:       
     Will the Minister of RAILWAYS be pleased to state: 
(a) whether it is a fact that Railways is still charging express train fare for passenger trains even 
though COVID-19 situation is now under control; 
 
 
(b) if so, the reasons therefor; 
 
 
(c) whether it is also a fact that Railways has increased passenger train fares by 200-300 per 
cen 
(d) whether Railways will resume all passenger train services as before COVID-19 pandemic to 
enhance local connectivity; and 
 
 
(e) if so, by when and if not, the reasons therefor? 
 
 
    
ANSWER 
 
 
   MINISTER OF RAILWAYS, COMMUNICATIONS AND  
ELECTRONICS & INFORMATION TECHNOLOGY 
 
 
                                           (SHRI ASHWINI VAISHNAW) 
 
(a) to (e): To contain the spread of Covid-19 Pandemic, Indian Railways had discontinued all 
regular passenger carrying trains with effect from 23 rd March 2020. Since November 2021, 
Mail/Express trains are being operated with regular number and as per rationalised time table. 
As on 20.07.2022, on a daily average basis, 9823 trains which include 1882 Mail/Express 
trains, 5651 Suburban trains and 2290 Passenger trains were operated over Indian Railways. 
Besides, passenger train services are being restored in a phased manner, subject to operational 
feasibility and commercial justification. 
Fare is charged as per categorisation of train service. Mail/Express train fare is higher 
than the ordinary train fare. If the train is categorised as Mail/Express, the same is charged as 
per Mail/Express fare.

GOVERNMENT OF INDIA 
MINISTRY OF RAILWAYS 
 
RAJYA SABHA 
UNSTARRED QUESTION NO. 1557 
ANSWERED ON 29.07.2022 
 
ROLLING OUT OF VANDE BHARAT EXPRESS TRAINS 
 
1557. SHRI VIVEK K. TANKHA : 
  
Will the Minister of RAILWAYS be pleased to state: 
(a) in how many phases Government is planning to roll out 400 Vande Bharat Express 
Trains announced in the 2022 Union Budget; 
 
(b) the current status of work completed in respect of the 75 Vande Bharat Express Trains 
announced by Government recently to connect all parts of the country; and 
 
(c) the number of cities in Madhya Pradesh that will benefit from the Vande Bharat Express 
Trains? 
 
ANSWER 
 
MINISTER OF RAILWAYS, COMMUNICATIONS AND  
ELECTRONICS & INFORMATION TECHNOLOGY  
 
 
(SHRI ASHWINI VAISHNAW) 
 
(a) The work of Manufacturing/Acquisition of 400 Energy Efficient Vande Bharat Rakes 
(Sleeper Version) has been planned to be executed in 2 Phases. 
(i) Under Phase-I: It is envisaged to manufacture 200 Vande Bharat sleeper rakes 
with stainless steel/ Aluminum body. 
(ii) Under Phase- II. a: One Hundred (100) Energy efficient Vande - Bharat rakes 
(sleeper version) with light weight Aluminum car body and distributed power 
system is envisaged to be manufactured. 
(iii) Under Phase- II.  b: One Hundred (100) Energy Efficient Vande-Bharat rakes with 
light weight Aluminum car body and concentrated power system are envisaged to 
be manufactured.  
(b) Production Units of Indian Railway have been entrusted with this task. They 
manufacture coaches according to  the Production Plan issued by Railway Board. Production 
plan of 2022-23 includes 75 Vande Bharat Train sets and production plan 2023-24 includes 
58 Vande Bharat Train sets be produced.  
(c) Indian Railways do not run train services, including Vande Bharat services, on State-wise 
basis as Railway network straddles across State boundaries. However, operation of more 
Vande Bharat trains, over Indian Railways network, is subject to operational feasibility, 
commercial justification and availability of rolling stock.

1559 BY SHRI BRIJLAL ANSWERED IN RAJYA SABHA ON 29.07.2022 REGARDING VIDEO 
SURVEILLANCE AT RAILWAY STATIONS 
    
(a)  to (c): Video Surveillance System through CCTV (Closed Circuit Television) cameras  is 
sanctioned at all 6490 stations (except halt stations) of Indian Railways. It includes 1050 stations 
sanctioned under Nirbhaya Fund and 194 stations under Integrated Security System.  As on date, 
861 stations are equipped with CCTV system. The station-wise details are appended. Indian 
Railways endeavours to gradually provide CCTV system at balance stations. No specific target 
date can be envisaged at this stage.

221

 
 
GOVERNMENT OF INDIA 
MINISTRY OF RAILWAYS 
 
RAJYA SABHA 
UNSTARRED QUESTION NO. 1560 
ANSWERED ON 29.07.2022 
 
 
                         400 VANDE BHARAT TRAINS 
 
      1560 # SHRI BRIJLAL: 
 
            Will the Minister of RAILWAYS be pleased to state: 
 
(a) whether Government has formulated a plan to operate four hundred Vande Bharat Trains to connect 
different cities of the country and if so, the details thereof; 
(b) the cities that are planned to be connected by these trains initially and the proposed changes in these 
trains for the convenience of passengers; and 
     (c) the expenditure involved in the operation of the these trains and the revenue likely to be earned by    
     Government from this? 
ANSWER 
, 
MINISTER OF RAILWAYS, COMMUNICATIONS AND  
ELECTRONICS & INFORMATION TECHNOLOGY 
 
(SHRI ASHWINI VAISHNAW) 
 
(a) and (b) In the Union Budget, 2022-23, announcement for 400 new generation Vande Bharat 
trains has been made. Vande Bharat services are State-of the-art Train –
set which have ultra 
modern features like quick acceleration, on board infotainment and Global Positioning System 
(GPS) based passenger information system, automatic sliding doors, retractable footsteps and 
Zero discharge vacuum bio toilets etc. Presently, 02 pairs of Vande Bharat Express namely, 
22435/22436 New Delhi-Varanasi Vande Bharat Express and 22439/22440 New Delhi-Shri 
Mata Vaishno Devi Katra Vande Bharat Express are in operation utilizing the available rake. 
Operation of more Vande Bharat trains, over Indian Railways network, is subject to operational 
feasibility, commercial justification and availability of rolling stock. 
(c) Nearly 400 rakes of Energy Efficient Vande Bharat trains with different technologies are 
planned to be manufactures at Indian Railways ’ Production Units with selected Technology 
Partner. The cost for manufacture of Energy Efficient Vande Bharat (Sleeper version) rakes has 
been estimated at ₹130 crores per rake under Rolling Stock Programme. 
The passenger earning of a train depends upon the composition of the train, the distance being 
covered, the profile of the train and its occupancy.  Since the proposed trains are yet to be 
introduced, it is difficult to estimate the likely revenue to be earned from these trains at this stage. 
***** 
222

GOVERNMENT OF INDIA 
MINISTRY OF RAILWAYS 
 
RAJYA SABHA 
UNSTARRED QUESTION NO. 1561 
 ANSWERED ON 29.07.2022 
 
DEVELOPMENT OF RAILWAY STATIONS IN PPP MODEL 
 
1561.  DR.  C.M. RAMESH: 
 
 Will the Minister of RAILWAYS be pleased to state: 
 
(a) the railway stations identified for development under Public Private Partnership (PPP) model , 
the State-wise details thereof; 
(b) the railway stations under renovation at present under PPP model, along with the timeline for 
their completion, the details thereof; 
and 
 (c) whether any Committee has been constituted for  recommending the railway stations to be 
developed under PPP model, if so, the details thereof and if not, the reasons therefor? 
 
ANSWER 
 
MINISTER OF RAILWAYS, COMMUNICATIONS AND 
ELECTRONICS & INFORMATION TECHNOLOGY  
 
(SHRI ASHWINI VAISHNAW) 
 
 
Redevelopment of Railway Stations at this scale is complex in nature and requires detailed techno-
economic feasibility studies and various statutory clearances from urban/local bodies etc. and these 
factors affect the completion time. Therefore, no time-frame can be indicated. 
**** 
 
(a) to (c): Ministry of Railways is conducting techno -economic feasibility studies at  various 
stations. Based on the outcome of these studies, stations are being taken up for 
development/redevelopment in phases under various models including Public Private Partnership 
(PPP). 
 
223

GOVERNMENT OF INDIA 
MINISTRY OF RAILWAYS 
 
RAJYA SABHA 
UNSTARRED QUESTION NO. 1562 
ANSWERED ON 29.07.2022 
 
NON STOPPING OF TRAINS AT KARPURIGRAM 
 
1562 # SHRI RAM NATH THAKUR: 
 
Will the Minister of RAILWAYS be pleased to state: 
 
(a) whether the stoppage of some trains at Karpurigram railway station under East Central Railways 
(ECR) Sonpur Division has been dropped; 
 
(b) if so, the trains, stoppages of which have been dropped after COVID-19 pandemic; 
 
(c) whether any study has been conducted to find out the inconvenience being faced by the local 
commuters due to non stopping of trains; 
 
(d) the grounds on which stoppage of trains at Karpurigram has been dropped; and 
 
(e) whether 
Government would consider resuming the dropped stoppages of trains at the said 
stations? 
ANSWER 
 
MINISTER OF RAILWAYS, COMMUNICATIONS AND  
ELECTRONICS & INFORMATION TECHNOLOGY 
 
(SHRI ASHWINI VAISHNAW) 
 
(a) to (e) Indian Railways, with the assistance of IIT-Bombay, have undertaken rationalization of 
time table, inter alia, to provide for better passenger safety by creating maintenance corridor blocks, 
minimizing conflicts in existing time table, etc. The exercise also included rationalization of 
stoppages. Accordingly, stoppages of 04 pairs of train services have been discontinued at 
Karpurigram station as these have been found operationally not expedient and commercially not 
justified.  However, provision of stoppage of trains is an on-going process on Indian Railways 
which is done keeping in view the operational feasibility, availability of alternative services, 
commercial justification etc.

234

 
 
STATEMENT REFERRED TO IN REPLY TO PARTS (a) TO (c) OF UNSTARRED 
QUESTION NO. 1570 BY SHRI VAIKO ANSWERED IN RAJYA SABHA ON 29.07.2022 
REGARDING SINGLE TRACK RAILWAY ROUTES IN TAMIL NADU 
 
(a) No, Sir. Trains on Indian Railways including those operating on single line sections, are charted 
at the Maximum Permissible Speed of the section and trains are scheduled accordingly. The dealy 
of trains cannot, therefore, be attributed to Single line tracks.  
 
(b) It is Railway’s endeavor to provide free potable water  at all grades of Railway Stations spread 
over the length and breadth of the country and adequate arrangements have been made to provide 
free potable water at all stations to meet the demand of drinking water facilities, including the 
single track railway routes of Tamil Nadu. 
Parking has been provided at all feasible locations over above single track railway routes based on 
the demand and patronage.  
Proper hygiene of toilets at stations over above single track railway routes is maintained by way of 
regular cleaning and regular inspections.  
Necessary security arrangements have been provided on these single track railway routes of Tamil 
Nadu. 
 
(c) Railway projects are sanctioned and taken up Zonal Railway-wise and not State-wise, as the 
Railway’s projects span across various State boundaries. Doub ling of projects are taken up on the 
basis of remuneration, augmentation of congested/saturated lines, Railways ’ own operational 
requirement, availability of resources, depending upon throwforward ongoing project and overall 
availability of funds. As on 01.04.2022, total 11 doubling projects of total length 1234 Km, costing 
₹14190 crore falling fully/partly in the State of Tamil Nadu are under different stages of 
planning/approval/execution.

236

STATEMENT REFERRED TO IN REPLY TO PARTS (a) TO (d) OF UNSTARRED 
QUESTION NO 1571 BY SHRI RAGHAV CHADHA AND DR ASHOK KUMAR MITTAL 
ANSWERED IN RAJYA SABHA ON 29.07.2022 REGARDING RAILWAY LINES IN 
PUNJAB  
 
(a)  During 2021-22, total length of Railway line in the State of Punjab is 2265 kms.  The 
Railway projects are sanctioned Zonal Railway-wise and not State-wise as the Railway projects 
may span across State boundaries. However, as on 01.04.2022, 12 Projects (6 New Lines and 6 
Doubling), costing ₹23,810 crore for 1,570 km length falling fully/partly in Punjab are under 
different stages of planning/approval/execution, out of which 159 Km length has been 
commissioned and an expenditure of ₹4,439 Cr. has been incurred upto March 2022.  These 
include : 
 6 New Line Projects of total length 356 km at a cost of ₹11,220 crore, out of which 61 
Km length has been commissioned and an expenditure of ₹2,322 Cr. has been incurred 
upto March 22. 
 6 Doubling Projects of total length 1,214 km at a cost of ₹12,590 crore, out of wh ich 
98Km length has been commissioned and an expenditure of ₹2,117 Cr. has been incurred 
upto March 22. 
      Since 2014, there has been substantial increase in Budget allocations and commensurate 
commissioning of infrastructure projects.  Average Annual Budget Allocation for infrastructure 
projects and safety works, falling fully/Partly in State of Punjab during 2014-19, has been 
enhanced to ₹1004 crore per year from ₹225 crore  per year during 2009-14, which is 346% 
more than  average annual budget allocation during 2009-14. These allocations have been 
increased to ₹1095 crore in financial year 2019-20 (387% more than average annual budget 
outlay of 2009-14), ₹1565 crore for financial year 2020-21 (596% more than average annual 
budget outlay of 2009-14), and ₹2262 crore for financial year 2021-22 (905% more than average 
annual budget outlay of 2009-14).  For financial year 2022-23, highest ever budget outlay of 
₹3,543 crore has been provided for these projects, which is 1475% more than average annual 
budget outlay of 2009-14. 
     The completion of any Railway project(s) depends on various factors like quick land 
acquisition by State Government, forest clearance by officials of forest department, deposition o f 
cost  by State Government in cost sharing projects, shifting of infringing utilities, statutory 
clearances from various authorities, geological and topographical conditions of area, law and 
order situation in the area of project(s) site, number of working months in a year for particular 
project site due to climatic considerations etc and all these factors affect the completion time of 
237

the project(s). With above constraints, every effort is being made to execute the project 
expeditiously. 
(b)      At present, Sahibzada Ajit Singh Nagar (Mohali) is being served by 24 Mail/Express (in 
single) train services and Rajpura is served by 55 Mail/Express (in Single) train services, which 
are adequately catering to the present level of traffic, offering at the stations. Besides, 
introduction of train services is an ongoing process on Indian Railways.  
(c)       Various steps taken by the Government for effective and speedy implementation of 
Railway projects include (i)setting up of Gati Shakti Directorate in the Ministry and Gati Shakti 
units in field, (ii) prioritisation of projects (iii) substantial increase in allocation of funds on 
priority projects (iv) delegation of powers at field level (v) close monitoring of progress of 
projects at various levels, and (vi) regular follow up with State Governments and concerned 
authorities for expeditious land acquisition, forestry and Wildlife clearances and for resolving 
other issues pertaining to projects. 
During 2014-22, 168 Km Doubling sections falling fully/partly in Punjab have been 
commissioned in the State at an average rate of 21 km per year. 
(d) Proposals for construction of Road Over Bridge/ Road Under Bridge (ROB/RUB) in lieu 
of Level Crossing(LCs) are either conceptualized by Zonal Railways or received from State 
Government and other Statutory Authorities. Af ter completing various formalities with State 
Government / Statutory Authorities regarding the consent for sharing of cost, closure of LCs and 
feasibility etc.,  proposals mutually agreed for construction of ROBs/RUBs in lieu of LCs are 
further processed fo r inclusion in  Railway Works Program me, which is a continuous 
process. Presently, no such proposal is under consideration from Punjab Government. However,  
27 Nos. ROB have been sanctioned in the State of Punjab.  
Upgradation/modernization of stations on Indian Railways is a continuous and on-going 
process. Works for improvement of passenger amenities including provision of Foot Over 
Bridges (FOBs) at stations are undertaken depending upon need, volume of passenger traffic and 
inter-se priority, subject to availability of funds. Presently, works for provision of FOBs at 22 
stations has been sanctioned and are under various stages of progress.

(d

*****
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
241

STATEMENT REFERRED TO IN REPLY TO PARTS (a) TO (d) OF UNSTARRED 
QUESTION NO.1573 ASKED BY SHRI AYODHYA RAMI REDDY ALLA ANSWERED 
IN RAJYA SABHA ON 29.07.2022 REGARDING SHARE OF RAILWAYS IN FREIGHT 
TRANSPORT 
 
(a): Indian Railways have prepared a National Rail Plan (NRP) for India – 2030. The 
objective of the plan is to create capacity ahead of demand, which in turn, would also cater to 
future growth in demand right up to 2050 and also increase the modal share of Railways to 
45% in freight traffic and continue to sustain it.  
 
In 2022-23, Indian Railways has set 1475 Million Tonnes as originating freight loading 
target. During April – June 2022, Indian Railways have achieved 378.80 Million Tonnes of 
originating freight loading, which is 7.15% more than the proportionate target for this period 
and 11.97% more than that achieved in the corresponding period of last year.  
 
(b): Regular compilation of rail and road shares in total freight movement is not done. Last 
time these were compiled by Rail India Technical and Economic Service Limited (RITES) as 
part of the National Rail Plan study and according to which the modal share of Railways was 
around 26% in 2017-18
. 
 
(c): Indian Railways has taken various steps to increase the share of the freight transport,
 such 
as Liberalized Automatic Freight Rebate policy in empty flow directions, concession in short 
lead traffic,  discount on loading of bagged consignment in open and flat wagons, 40% 
discount in freight to fly ash traffic, Station to Station rate (STS), 15% concession on 
movement of empty containers and empty flat wagons, de-notification of large number of 
commodities for expansion of  container freight basket, Round trip charges on ultra short lead 
container traffic.   
 
Two Dedicated Freight Corridors (Western and Eastern Dedicated Freight Corridors) have 
been taken up to create adequate capacity, which is essential requirement to cater to the 
traffic demand and improve quality of service. 
 
Indian Railways have also introduced wagon investment schemes such as General Purpose 
Wagons Investment Scheme (GPWIS) for procurement of general purpose wagons by private 
242

parties, Liberalized Special Freight Train Operator Scheme (LSFTO) for procurement of 
Special purpose/high capacity wagons, Automobile Freight Trains Operator Scheme (AFTO) 
for investment in Automobile carrier wagons, etc.    
 
A new Gati Shakti Multi-Modal Cargo Terminal (GCT) policy to boost investment from 
industry in development of additional terminals for handling rail cargo has also been 
launched on 15.12.2021. 
 
(d): The volume of freight traffic has increased during the last three years, which is as under:

cost 
sharing projects.  
 
Since 2014, there has been substantial increase in budget allocation and commensurate 
commissioning of infrastructure projects. Average annual budget allocation for infrastructure 
projects and safety works, falling fully/partly in the State of Andhra Pradesh, during 2014-19 has 
increased to ₹2830 crore  per year from ₹886 crore per year during 2009-14. Thus, an increase of 
219% over the average annual budget allocation of 2009-14. Budget outlay for these projects has 
been increased to ₹3885 crore in 2019 -20 (338% more than average annual budget outlay during 
2009-14), ₹4910 crore in 2020-21 (454% more than average annual budget  outlay during 2009-

14) and ₹6223 crore for financial year 2021-22 (602% more than average annual outlay during

In 2014-22, 989 Km sections (350 km of New line and 639 km of Doubling) falling fully/partly 
in Andhra Pradesh have been commissioned at an average rate of 123.63 km per year, which is 
70% more than commissioning during 2009-14( 72.6 Km/Yr.)

(a) & (c) The completion of Electrification project depends upon various factors like clearances 
from the concerned Departments/State Governments/Central Ministries, completion of 
Transmission Line works by State Power Utilities and resolution of law & order issues during 
execution, which are beyond the control of Ministry of Railways. Therefore, it is not feasible to 
fix time lines for completion of the projects.

(OSOP) scheme of Indian Railways.  
 
  The One Station One Product policy issued vide Commercial Circular 12 of 2022 ha s the 
objective to promote ‘Vocal for Local’ vision of the Government of India, provide a market for 
indigenous product and opportunity to railway passengers to experience the rich heritage of India 
and to buy local/indigenous products. It is also envisage d to create additional income 
opportunities for the marginalised sections of society. 
 
  The scheme envisages showcasing, selling and giving high visibility to indigenous/local 
level products which may include handicraft/artefacts, textiles and handlooms, traditional 
garments, local agricultural produce/processed/semi processed foods. 
  .....2/-

-2- 
  Sale outlets at Railway Stations across the country are being set up for selling these 
products which would provide opportunities for enhanced livelihood through skill development 
to local artisans, potters, weavers/ handloom weavers, craftsmen etc.

station under OSOP scheme. ‘Etikoppaka Lacquer and wooden toys manufacture rs aided co-
operative society’ is currently operating the OSOP stall at Platform no 1 (concourse area) of the 
station. As such, the scheme has been providing opportunities to the local artisans/ craftsmen of 
the area around Visakhapatnam to showcase and sell their products to rail travellers. 
 
(d): Yes, Sir. One Station One Product Scheme (OSOP) outlet at Visakhapatnam is being 
continued. 
 
(e): Does not arise.
